Detailed coordinate descent:
- Keep a normalized sequence (sum=1). For each coordinate i, optimize value x_i while keeping sum 1 by scaling others. Let S = sum a. If choose new a_i = x (0≤x≤S), set others = old_others * (S - x)/S_old? Wait old sum S. If old a_i = x0, others sum S_o=S-x0. After setting x, to keep total S (e.g., 1), others scale factor = (S - x)/(S - x0). If S=1. The function f(x) is smooth. We can do golden section in interval [0,S] or perhaps [0,2*x0, S]? Since optimum likely near. But objective max of rational quadratics may have multiple minima. We can also do discrete candidate x values: 0, x0/2, x0*1.5, etc. Golden section assumes unimodal; may fail. But we can combine with local random sampling.

- For each coordinate, to find good x, sample a grid around x0 e.g. [0, x0*(1±0.5), x0*(2,0.5), S*0.05, x0] plus perhaps second-order Newton? Then refine best with scalar minimization using `minimize_scalar`? Can use scipy.optimize.minimize_scalar on bracket. But no closures. It's still nested if define inside. Avoid nested definitions. We can implement our own ternary/golden inline.

- But scaling all others for each x requires modifying array. We can create temporary array copy for each x, scale, evaluate. n=128, copy cheap. For n=1024, copying and eval many maybe heavy. Use internal or restrict.

- Because objective scale invariant, we can maintain sum=1. For coordinate i: old_x = a[i], other_sum = S - old_x. For candidate x, factor = (S - x)/other_sum if other_sum>0 else new array with one nonzero? If x= S and others zero, sequence single spike. Evaluate. Candidate x near 0. Alternative parameterization: maintain log weights y; move one coordinate in y by δ: y_i += δ, then renormalize a=softmax(y). This changes relative weights only, no explicit scaling. Objective smooth. We can do coordinate line search on y_i. Because scale invariant, y_i absolute offset irrelevant; set mean y=0. For coordinate i, log-normalized update: a ∝ exp(y). Changing y_i by δ multiplies a_i relative to others by e^δ, then renormalizes. Candidate factor for a_i relative: if y_i changes δ, new a_i = a_i e^δ / (a_i e^δ + other_sum). Let's design `optimize_smooth(z0, t, time_limit, maxiter=...)`:
```
def smooth_value_and_grad(z, t):
    a = softmax(z)
    b = fft_conv(a)
    m = b.max()
    # For numerical stability: scaled = t*(b-m)
    expb = np.exp(t*(b-m) - logsumexp(t*(b-m))) maybe p
    p = softmax(t*b)
    val = 2*n*(m + (1/t)*logsumexp(t*(b-m)))  # smooth upper approx of max? Actually (1/t)logsumexp(t b) = m + (1/t) logsumexp(t(b-m)).
    gradA = 2*np.correlate(p, a, mode='valid')
    mean_g = np.dot(a, gradA)
    gradZ = a*(gradA - mean_g)
    return val, gradZ
```

Design `optimize_deep(a0, time_limit, seed, max_lens?)`:
Pseudo:
```
def optimize_deep(a0, time_limit, seed=0):
    rng = np.random.default_rng(seed)
    a = normalize(a0)
    n = len(a)
    score = safe_eval(a)
    best = ...
    deadline = time + time_limit
    # Optional: try simple transformations (reverse, sym, blur) keep if better
    for transform in [reverse, sym, gaussian blur 2, sort?]:
         cand = transform(a); if score better update.
    # LP phase
    if n <= 512 and linprog available and remaining:
        iterations=0
        while remaining>... and iterations<100:
            h = lp_direction(a)
            if h is None: break
            improve = lp_line_search_one(a,h); iterations++
            if no improve: break
    # L-BFGS anneal
    for c in [10,30,100,300,1000,3000,10000,20000]:
        if remaining < 3: break
        bmax = conv_max(a)
        lam = c / max(bmax,1e-12)
        z = log(a) - mean
        # run minimize with maxiter maybe 80, but time cap by checking? No time cap. We can run one call; it should be quick.
        res = minimize(...)
        z = res.x
        a = softmax(z)
        score = real(...); if improve best
        # Additionally run with higher lam maybe from same a
    # After L-BFGS, coordinate polish if n<=512
    coord = coordinate_descent(a, remaining*0.2)
        # maybe multiple small eps ratios
    # Basin hop: while remaining, perturb best and run L-BFGS moderate temps.
    for _ in range(max(1, remaining//100)):
         a_pert = normalize(best * exp(rng.normal(0,sigma,n)))
         quick_LBFGS(a_pert, temps [100,1000])
         update
    return best
```

Need be careful: `minimize` may return poor z if fails. We can compare value. But we evaluate real score and keep best separately.

`lp_direction` dense building each iteration O(n^2) for matrix: 2n-1 rows, n cols. Can build using loops or vectorization. n=128 no issue. For n=512, 262k entries, okay. Use `C = np.zeros((2*n-1,n), dtype=float)`. For j in 0..n-1, valid r from j to j+n-1, C[j:j+n, j] = a? Wait C[r,j]=a[r-j] if 0≤r-j<n. For fixed j, r-j = l=0..n-1 => C[j+l,j]=a[l]. ```

# For each j, column is zeros except slice [j:j+n]=a

```

Functions:

```
def _clip_norm(a):
    a=np.asarray(a,dtype=float)
    a[a<0]=0
    a[~np.isfinite(a)]=0
    s=a.sum()
    if s<=1e-15:
       return np.ones(len(a))/len(a)
    return a/s
```
Name top-level no underscore? okay.

`safe_eval(list_or_array)`:
```
def safe_eval(a):
    try:
       lst=[float(x) for x in a.tolist()]
    except: return inf
    if not lst: return inf
    try:
       val=evaluate_sequence(lst)
    except: return inf
    try: val=float(val)
    except: return inf
    if not math.isfinite(val): return inf
    return val
```
But converting every eval to list for n=1024 overhead. Could just `evaluate_sequence(a.tolist())`. Need ensure finite. Good.

`fft_conv(a)`:
```
def fft_conv(a):
   n=len(a)
   size=2*n-1
   L=1 << (size-1).bit_length()
   A=np.fft.rfft(a, L)
   c=np.fft.irfft(A*A, L)[:size]
   c[c<0] = 0.0  # suppress
   return c
```
For n=1, size=1, L=1? `(size-1).bit_length()` if size=1 -> bit_length 0, 1<<0=1. okay. But rfft length1 okay.

`softmax(z)`:
```
def softmax_z(z):
   z=z - np.max(z)
   e=np.exp(z)
   s=e.sum()
   if s<=0 or not finite: return np.ones(len(z))/len(z)
   return e/s
```

`log_vector(a, eps=1e-300)`:
```
def log_vector(a):
   a=np.asarray(a,float); a=np.maximum(a,1e-300)
   z=np.log(a)
   return z - np.mean(z)
```

`smooth_value(z, lam, n)`:

# scaled max 0, sum exp
se=np.exp(scaled)
s=se.sum()
if s<=0: lse=0.0
else: lse=np.log(s)
val=2.0*n*(m + lse/lam)
return val
```

def lbfgs_step(a, lam, maxiter=80):
   if not HAVE_SCIPY_OPT: return a
   z=log_vector(a)
   res = minimize(smooth_value, z, method='L-BFGS-B', args=(lam,), jac=smooth_grad, options={'maxiter': maxiter, 'ftol':1e-12, 'gtol':1e-8, 'maxls':50})

`anneal_lbfgs(a, time_budget, c_schedule)`:
- algorithm:
```

  if time>deadline: break
  bmax = fft_conv(a).max()

`lp_improve(a, time_budget, max_iter)`:

`lp_direction(a)`:
- if n > 2048 skip; if linprog unavailable skip.
- Build C. `obj = -np.ones(n)`. `b = np.ones(2*n-1)`.
- For numerical, scale b? C entries up to 1 (normalized a). b=1. Some rows may be very small; no issue. But if a has tiny values, constraints loose; h may huge; normalize.
- Could improve conditioning by scaling each row by max(C[row])? Constraint C h ≤1. If a row max small, constraint loose; okay. But LP may have large h. Normalization removes. However huge h can cause ill-conditioning; scale rows by positive sum to make active. We can set `b = np.maximum(np.sum(C, axis=1), 1e-12)` maybe? The state-of-art uses bound M, not arbitrary. Using b=1 is equivalent to M if C scaled by 1/M. Row scaling changes relative constraints, thus direction. Need choose correct scaling. We want all constraints f*h ≤ M_current. If current M = max(a*a). We can set `A = C / M` and b=1. But if M known. Using b=1 without scaling is equivalent to M=1, much looser; direction different. In state-of-art, constraints all ≤M, same scaling for all rows. So we should use M. If M ~0.006, C/M entries up to ~160 maybe; b=1. Or scale C by (1/M). Could also divide each row by M? Same. def try_simple_moves(a, score):
   best_sc=score; best=a

`coordinate_descent(a, time_budget, max_sweeps=?)`:
- Use real eval. For n maybe >512, reduce.
```
def coordinate_descent(a, time_budget):
   n=len(a); best_sc=safe_eval(a); best=a.copy()
   if n>1024: return best
   ratios=[0.0,0.25,0.5,0.75,1.25,1.6,2.25]
   deadline=time+time_budget
   sweeps=0
   while time < deadline and sweeps<30:
      improved=False
      for i in range(n):
         old=best[i]; other=1-old (best sum1)
         candxs = [0.0, old*0.25, old*0.5, old*0.75, old*1.25, old*1.6, 1.0]
         # unique filter
         for x in candxs:
            if x<=0 and old==0: continue? include 0? If x=0 same maybe skip.
            if x<0 or x>1: continue
            if abs(x-old)<1e-12: continue
            if deadline check break maybe every few
            temp=best.copy()
            if other > 1e-15:
                temp *= (1.0 - x)/other
                temp[i]=x
            else:
                temp=np.zeros(n); temp[i]=1.0
            sc=safe_eval(temp)
            if sc < best_sc - 1e-12:
                best=temp; best_sc=sc; improved=True
      sweeps+=1
      if not improved: break
   return best
```
This uses current best sum1. If other very small, scaling overflow? x near1; factor ~1/other could huge but temp entries old*factor = old*(1-x)/other; for old=other? If other=1e-16, old≈1, x cannot be much less than 1 without factor huge; but normalized entries finite. We can skip if other<1e-12 unless x=1. Good.

But copy+scale for each candidate O(n). For n=128 okay. For n=512 maybe 7*512*512? Actually candidates 7*512=3584 copies per sweep, each 512 array ~1.8M floats, 14MB plus eval; 10 sweeps 140MB, okay. But eval direct O(n^2) 262k*35k=9B too slow. So for n=512 limit candidates/sweeps. Use if n<=256 for full coordinate; n=512 small 2 sweeps maybe with 3 candidates. We'll condition:
- if n<=256: ratios full, max sweeps 20
- elif n<=512: ratios [0,0.5,1.5], sweeps 5
- else skip.

```
def generate_inits(n, parent):
    res=[]
    x=np.linspace(0.1,0.9,n) maybe avoid exact zeros?
    res.append(np.ones(n)/n)
    # linear skew
    for p in [0.9,0.7,0.5,0.3,0.1]: # left endpoint relative to average 1? q=2-p
       a=np.linspace(p, 2.0-p, n)
       a=np.clip(a,0,None); normalize; append
    # exponential
    for alpha in [-4,-2,-1,1,2,4]:
       a=np.exp(alpha*(np.linspace(0,1,n)-0.5)); normalize
    # power
    for gamma in [0.3,1,3,10]:
       a=np.linspace(1e-3,1,n)**gamma; normalize; also reverse
    # beta shapes
    for a1,b1 in [(0.5,2),(0.5,0.5),(2,0.5),(1,3),(3,1),(0.8,1.5),(1.5,0.8)]:
       a=(np.linspace(1e-4,1-1e-4,n))**(a1-1) * (1-np.linspace(...))**(b1-1)
 # two-hump? 
    res.append(gaussian centered)
 # parent
    if parent is not None:
       if len(parent)==n: append parent
       else: p=np.interp(np.linspace(0,1,n), np.linspace(0,1,len(parent)), parent); append
    # random Dirichlet with conc 0.2,0.6,2
    for conc in [0.2,0.6,2.0]: append rng.dirichlet(np.full(n,conc))
    return res
```

Let's write helper functions for shapes:
```
def init_linear(n, p):
   q=2.0-p
   a=np.linspace(p,q,n)
   a=np.clip(a,0,None)
   return normalize(a)
```
But for discrete, integral trapezoid: endpoints p,q normalized anyway. If p=1.8,q=0.2; sequence decreasing.
```
def init_exp(n, alpha):
   x=np.linspace(0,1,n)
   a=np.exp(alpha*(x-0.5))
   return normalize(a)
```
```
def init_power(n, gamma, rev=False):
   x=np.linspace(1e-3,1,n)
   a=x**gamma
   if rev: a=a[::-1]
   return normalize(a)
```
```
def init_beta(n, a1, b1):
   x=np.linspace(1e-4,1-1e-4,n)
   a=x**(a1-1)*(1-x)**(b1-1)
   return normalize(a)
```
```
def init_gauss(n, c, s):
   x=np.linspace(0,1,n); a=np.exp(-0.5*((x-c)/s)**2); return normalize
```
Maybe too many.

`normalize` returns zeros/n if empty? If empty, return ones(1)? But sequences nonempty. Implement:
```
def normalize(a):
   a=np.asarray(a,dtype=float)
   if a.ndim!=1 or a.size==0: return np.ones(1)
   a=np.where(np.isfinite(a), a, 0.0)
   a=np.maximum(a,0.0)
   s=a.sum()
   if s <= 1e-15:
       return np.ones(a.size)/a.size
   return a/s
```
This modifies sum to 1.

Now, let's consider the LP direction with normalized a sum1. It maximizes sum h subject to (a*h) ≤ M. But our M from a*a. If M is very small (target 0.00587), A entries up to 1/M ~170. B=1. `lp_direction(a)` code:
```
def lp_direction(a):
    if not HAVE_SCIPY_OPT: return None
    n=len(a); m=2*n-1
    b=fft_conv(a); M=float(b.max())
    if M <= 1e-300: return None
    A=np.zeros((m,n), dtype=float)
    for j in range(n):
        A[j:j+n, j] = a
    A /= M
    # Remove rows that are all zero? not necessary but can reduce
    rowsum=np.sum(A, axis=1)
    keep = rowsum > 1e-15
    if not np.all(keep): A=A[keep]
    try:
       res = linprog(c=-np.ones(n), A_ub=A, b_ub=np.ones(A.shape[0]), bounds=(0,None), method='highs', options={'disp': False, 'presolve': True})
       if not res.success: return None
       h=res.x
       h=np.asarray(h,float)
       h=np.where(np.isfinite(h),h,0.0); h=np.maximum(h,0.0)
       s=h.sum()
       if s <= 1e-15: return None
       return h/s
    except Exception:
       return None
```
Rows with all zero: If a has all zeros? no. If keep removes all? no.

Potential condition: `linprog` with A_ub having large values up to 170, b=1. okay. If M very close to 0 but normalized sum1 impossible? If n huge and uniform normalized a ~1/n, M~1/n ~0.0005 for n=2000, A ~2000, okay. For n=512, M~0.004, A~250. OK.

for it in range(max_iter):
   if time>dl: break
   h=lp_direction(a)
   if h is None: break
   best=a; best_sc=score
   for t in [0.1,0.25,0.4,0.6,0.8,1.0]:
       if time>dl: break
       cand=(1-t)*a + t*h
       sc=safe_eval(cand)
       if sc<best_sc: best=cand;best_sc=sc
   if best_sc < score - 1e-12:
       a=normalize(best); score=best_sc; update_holder(holder,a,score,label)
       continue
   # If no improvement, maybe try t very small 0.05? or break

return a
```

Now, `lbfgs_step` with `maxiter` maybe 0 if no scipy. Fallback mirror. Implement fallback `mirror_lbfgs(a, lam, steps)`:
```
def mirror_fb(a, lam, steps):
  for step in range(steps):
    b=fft_conv(a); m=b.max(); if m<1e-30: break
    scaled=lam*(b-m); se=np.exp(scaled); s=se.sum(); p=se/s if s>0 else onehot
    gradA=4.0*len(a)*np.correlate(p,a,'valid')
    # gradient in z: a*(gradA - a·gradA)
    g=gradA - np.dot(a,gradA)
    eta=0.05
    lf=-eta*g
    lf -= lf.max()
    a=a*np.exp(lf)
    a=normalize(a)
  return a
```
This updates z in our surrogate gradient direction? For natural gradients, factor okay. It may not be scaled with lam? lam inside p. Good.

In `lbfgs_step`, if no scipy, call `mirror_fb(a, lam, steps=20)`.

Now, `normalize` uses `np.where(np.isfinite(a), a, 0.0)`. If a object? asarray float may fail; catch? In safe_eval, try. `normalize` can catch and return ones.
```
def normalize(arr):
    try: a=np.asarray(arr,dtype=float)
    except: return np.ones(1)
    if a.ndim !=1: a=a.ravel()
    if a.size==0: return np.ones(1)
    a=np.where(np.isfinite(a), a, 0.0)
    a=np.maximum(a,0.0)
    s=float(a.sum())
    if not math.isfinite(s) or s <= 1e-15:
        return np.ones(a.size)/a.size
    return a/s
```
Good.

`safe_eval` uses `normalize` then `evaluate_sequence(list)`. If evaluate_sequence not defined in final test? It is. If somehow not, return inf? But task says available. We can catch NameError.
```
def safe_eval(a):
   try:
      arr=normalize(a)
      if arr.size==0 or arr.sum()<0.01: return inf
      val=evaluate_sequence([float(x) for x in arr])
      val=float(val)
      if math.isfinite(val): return val
   except Exception:
      pass
   return inf
```
This normalizes each eval, overhead but okay. If hidden function uses unnormalized cap? We normalize sum1; okay.

But if best sequence from parent has entries >1000, normalizing changes from clamped score? Hidden likely no. Fine.

`fast_score(a)`:
```
def fast_score(a):
   arr=normalize(a)
   n=len(arr); if n==0 return inf
   b=fft_conv(arr); mx=float(b.max())
   return 2.0*n*mx
```
`fast_max_conv(a)` returns b.max without score.

Now, `fft_conv` for a normalized sum1 but entries small. It clips negative to 0, but due FFT, sum b≈1. If n large and a sparse, okay. Need `a` float. If not, asarray.
```
def fft_conv(a):
   a=np.asarray(a,dtype=float)
   n=a.size
   if n==0: return np.array([0.0])
   size=2*n-1
   L=1 << (size-1).bit_length()
   A=np.fft.rfft(a,L)
   c=np.fft.irfft(A*A,L)[:size]
   c=np.where(np.isfinite(c), c, 0.0)
   c[c<0]=0.0
   return c
```
For size=1, `(size-1).bit_length()` 0, L=1. Good.

`smooth_value` using `lam` maybe huge; computing `scaled=lam*(b-m)` where b-m negative, lam huge could underflow to -inf; exp handles. If lam*m? no. If `lam` is inf? Ensure finite. In anneal, if bmax<1e-15 set m=1. Use `lam = c / max(float(bmax), 1e-300)`. If bmax tiny, lam huge maybe 1e300, scaled products inf. Limit lam max: `lam = min(c / max(bmax,1e-300), 1e6)`? For b ~0.006, c=1e4 -> 1.6e6; scaled max -1.6e6, underflow but not inf. 1e6 okay. For bmax=1e-12 and c=1, lam=1e12, scaled can be -1e12, exp underflow; no inf but multiplication may produce -1e12 finite (float can). If lam >1e308, inf. Limit to 1e8? If bmax <1e-8 and c=1, lam=1e8. Use `lam = min(c / max(bmax,1e-15), 1e8)`. This may underfocus for very small b, but okay. Or normalize b by bmax: Instead of fixed lam, we can implement smooth function with a parameter `c` that uses `m` inside and gradient adjusted? Earlier concern non-smooth m, but we can compute p via `softmax(c*(b-m)/m)` and value/gradient can use m? The derivative of this non-smooth expression? It equals derivative of logsumexp(c b / m? No if m variable in λ. If we implement p=softmax(c*(b-m)/m) = softmax(c*b/m - c). If m were constant, λ=c/m. For gradient, if we ignore derivative of λ, wrong. But for practical, using fixed lam outside is safer. Limit lam to 1e8, for bmax small focus maybe all? If bmax 1e-8 and lam1e8, max log c=1, okay. Good.

In schedule, c up to 1000. If bmax=0.006, lam=166k, below 1e8. If bmax=1e-5 for n=2000, lam=1e8 for c=1000? 1000/1e-5=1e8. okay.

Now, `lbfgs_step`: If `minimize` fails, fallback. Need options: `gtol` maybe too strict for high lam. Use `gtol=1e-6`, `ftol=1e-10`. `maxfun` maybe? We can set `maxfun = maxiter*50`? Not necessary.
```
def lbfgs_step(a, lam, maxiter=100):
   a=normalize(a)
   if maxiter<=0: return a
   if HAVE_MINIMIZE:
      z=log_vector(a)
      try:
         res=minimize(smooth_value, z, method='L-BFGS-B', args=(lam,), jac=smooth_grad,
                      options={'maxiter': int(maxiter), 'ftol':1e-12, 'gtol':1e-8, 'maxls':50})
         if res.x is not None:
             a2=softmax_z(res.x)
             if fast_score(a2) < fast_score(a)+1e-9 or True:
                 return a2
      except Exception:
         pass
   return mirror_fb(a, lam, 20)
```
If `res` value worse, still return; caller uses fast. Good.

Maybe L-BFGS with high lam and starting from z mean0 can get stuck at uniform if gradient zero. In anneal, we add noise if `np.max(a)/np.mean(a) < 1.02`. But if exactly uniform and c=0.1, gradient maybe tiny but nonzero due numerical? Add noise.

`mirror_fb`: steps maybe 30. Use eta schedule? Could use eta=0.1. To avoid overshooting, line search? Not needed.

Now, `anneal_lbfgs`: It calls `fast_max_conv` and `fast_score` multiple times. Need pass `rng`.
```
def anneal_lbfgs(a, budget, holder, rng, label, c_schedule, maxiter_base):
   a=normalize(a)
   best=a.copy(); best_sc=safe_eval(a); update_holder(holder,best,best_sc,label)
   dl=time.monotonic()+budget
   cur=a.copy()
   fast_cur=fast_score(cur)
   fast_best=fast_score(best)
   for c in c_schedule:
      if time.monotonic() > dl: break
      bmax=fast_max_conv(cur)
      if bmax <= 1e-15: break
      lam = c / max(bmax, 1e-15)
      lam = min(lam, 1e8)
      start=cur
      if fast_best < fast_cur:
          start=best
      if np.max(start) / max(float(np.mean(start)),1e-15) < 1.03:
          start=add_noise(start, rng, 0.1)
      maxit = maxiter_base
      # maybe reduce if time remaining small? not
      a2=lbfgs_step(start, lam, maxit)
      sc2=safe_eval(a2)
      if np.isfinite(sc2) and sc2 < best_sc:
          best=a2; best_sc=sc2; cur=a2; fast_cur=fast_score(cur); fast_best=fast_score(best); update_holder(...)
      else:
          f2=fast_score(a2)
          if f2 < fast_cur:
              cur=a2; fast_cur=f2
          # else keep cur
   # final maybe if cur fast improved but real not, evaluate? already
   return best, best_sc
```
If `lbfgs_step` returns same due failure, continue. `fast_best` update if new best.

Maybe if budget small, `maxiter_base` should scale: `maxit = min(maxiter_base, max(5, int(50 * (dl-time)/max(1,budget))))`? Simpler fixed.

`coordinate_descent` time check inside inner loop every few candidates to avoid overrun.
```
def coordinate_descent(a, budget, holder, label):
   a=normalize(a); best=a; best_sc=safe_eval(a); update_holder
   n=len(a)
   if n>512 or budget<1: return best
   if n<=128: ratios=[0.0,0.4,0.7,1.0,1.3,1.7,2.2]; max_sweeps=50
   elif n<=256: ratios=[0.0,0.5,1.5]; max_sweeps=12
   else: ratios=[0.0,0.5]; max_sweeps=4
   dl=time+budget
   sweeps=0
   while sweeps<max_sweeps and time<dl:
      improved=False
      for i in range(n):
         if time>dl: break
         old=best[i]
         other=1.0-old
         # candidate xs unique
         xs=[0.0, old*0.4, old*0.7, old*1.3, old*1.7, 1.0]
         # filter finite, 0<=x<=1, not close old
         for x in xs:
            if time>dl: break
            if x < -1e-12 or x > 1+1e-12: continue
            x=max(0,min(1,x))
            if abs(x-old) <= 1e-12: continue
            if other <= 1e-12:
                if x < 1-1e-12: continue
                temp=np.zeros(n); temp[i]=1.0
            else:
                temp=best.copy()
                temp *= (1.0-x)/other
                temp[i]=x
            sc=safe_eval(temp)
            if np.isfinite(sc) and sc < best_sc - 1e-12:
                best=temp; best_sc=sc; improved=True
                update_holder(holder,best,best_sc,label)
      sweeps+=1
      if not improved: break
   return best
```
When `x=1.0` and other tiny, factor 0, temp zeros except i. okay.

Potential issue: `temp *= factor` scales all entries; if factor huge (old near1, x not near1) maybe overflow? We skip if other <=1e-12. If other=1e-8, x=0, factor=1e8, other entries old*1e8 ≈1? Actually old others may be ~1e-8, finite. okay.

Now, `simple_moves` uses random perms. It should call `safe_eval`. It can update holder. It returns best. For vertical, normalize. Need `rng` can be None.
```
def simple_moves(a, score, holder, rng, max_evals=10, label="move"):
   a=normalize(a); best=a; best_sc=score
   update_holder(holder,best,best_sc,label)
   evals=0
   n=len(a)
   moves=[]
   if n>1:
      moves.append(a[::-1])
      moves.append(0.5*(a+a[::-1]))
   if n>2:
      moves.append(blur1d(a))
   s=np.sort(a)
   moves.extend([s, s[::-1], zigzag(a)])
   for raw in moves:
      if evals>=max_evals: break
      cand=normalize(raw); evals+=1
      sc=safe_eval(cand)
      if np.isfinite(sc) and sc<best_sc:
         best=cand; best_sc=sc; update_holder(...)
   if rng is not None:
      for _ in range(max(0, max_evals-evals)):
         cand=a.copy(); rng.shuffle(cand); cand=normalize(cand)
         sc=safe_eval(cand)
         if ... update
   return best,best_sc
```
`zigzag` top-level:
```
def zigzag(a):
   n=len(a); d=np.sort(a)[::-1]
   out=np.zeros(n)
   pos_even=np.arange(0,n,2)
   pos_odd=np.arange(1,n,2)
   k_even=min(len(pos_even), len(d))
   k_odd=min(len(pos_odd), max(0,len(d)-k_even))
   out[pos_even[:k_even]]=d[:k_even]
   out[pos_odd[:k_odd]]=d[k_even:k_even+k_odd]
   return normalize(out)
```
This places larger at even, next at odd. Maybe okay.

`blur1d`: `np.convolve(a, [1,2,1], mode='same')` for even length may shift but fine.

Now, `quick_optimize` will call `simple_moves` maybe with max_evals based on time. Then LP, anneal.

```
def quick_optimize(a0, budget, holder, rng, label):
   if budget <= 0: return safe_eval(a0)
   a=normalize(a0); sc=safe_eval(a); update_holder(holder,a,sc,label)
   dl=time.monotonic()+budget
   n=len(a)
   # simple moves if time and n<=512
   if n<=512 and time<dl:
       a,sc=simple_moves(a,sc,holder,rng,max_evals=6,label=label+" simple")
   # LP if n<=256 and budget>3
   if HAVE_LINPROG and n<=256 and (dl-time)>1:
       lp_time=min(2.0, (dl-time)*0.25, 5.0)
       a=lp_improve(a, lp_time, holder, label+" lp")
   # LBFGS quick temps
   c_schedule=[0.3,3,30,300]
   if (dl-time)>1:
       a,sc=anneal_lbfgs(a, (dl-time)*0.8, holder, rng, label+" lbfgs", c_schedule, maxiter_base=(60 if n<=128 else 30))
   return a
```
Need if `anneal_lbfgs` uses remaining budget; okay.

But `quick_optimize` during raw scan: we won't call for all raw, only top. Good.

`deep_optimize(a0, budget, holder, rng, label)`:
```
def deep_optimize(a0,budget,holder,rng,label):
   a=normalize(a0); sc=safe_eval(a); update
   dl=time+budget
   n=len(a)
   # simple moves (more perms)
   if n<=512:
      a,sc=simple_moves(a,sc,holder,rng,max_evals=16,label)
   # LP phase 1
   if HAVE_LINPROG and n<=512:
      t_lp = min(30.0, max(0.5,(dl-time)*0.15))
      a=lp_improve(a,t_lp,holder,label)
   # LBFGS anneal
   c_schedule=[0.1,0.3,1,3,10,30,100,300,1000,3000,10000]
   if (dl-time)>2:
      a,sc=anneal_lbfgs(a, (dl-time)*0.45, holder,rng,label, c_schedule, maxiter_base=(200 if n<=128 else 100 if n<=256 else 40))
   # simple moves after
   if n<=512 and (dl-time)>1:
      a,sc=simple_moves(a,sc,holder,rng,max_evals=12,label)
   # LP phase 2
   if HAVE_LINPROG and n<=512 and (dl-time)>2:
      a=lp_improve(a, min(20,(dl-time)*0.15), holder, label)
   # coordinate
   if (dl-time)>1:
      a=coordinate_descent(a, min(30,(dl-time)*0.2), holder, label)
   # final small anneal high temps
   if (dl-time)>5:
      a,sc=anneal_lbfgs(a, (dl-time)*0.5, holder,rng,label, [100,300,1000,3000,10000], maxiter_base=most)
   # basin hops
   if (dl-time)>20 and n<=256:
      sigmas=[0.05,0.1,0.2,0.4]
      sidx=0
      while time<dl-10:
         hop_budget=min(40, (dl-time)//2) # integer? use float
         if hop_budget <5: break
         a_pert=add_noise(a,rng,sigmas[sidx%4]); sidx+=1
         # quick optimize hop with no LP? use anneal low temps
         a,sc=anneal_lbfgs(a_pert, hop_budget, holder,rng,label+" hop", [10,100,1000], maxiter_base=80)
         if (dl-time)<20: break
   return a
```

max_hops=5
for hop in range(max_hops): if time... break; hop_budget = min(60,(dl-time)/max(1,max_hops-hop))
```

```
def parent_to_length(parent, n):
    p=normalize(parent)
    if p.size == n: return p
    if p.size == 1: return np.full(n, p[0])
    xold=np.linspace(0.0,1.0,p.size)
    xnew=np.linspace(0.0,1.0,n)
    q=np.interp(xnew, xold, p)
    return normalize(q)
```

`init_linear`: q=2-p; if p>2? p range 0..2. Ensure nonnegative.
```
def init_linear(n,p):
   if n<=1: return np.ones(n)
   q=2.0-p
   a=np.linspace(p,q,n)
   a=np.clip(a,0.0,None)
   if a.sum()<=0: a=np.linspace(1,n,n)
   return normalize(a)
```
`init_exp`: if alpha large, exp may overflow? alpha up to 4, range .5, exp2 fine. `np.exp(alpha * (x - 0.5))`.

`init_beta`: x endpoints 1e-4. If exponents negative, values large but finite. Normalize.

Now, let's refine the objective gradient factor with value. `smooth_value`: `val = 2.0 * n * (m + (lse / lam))`. For p=softmax(lam*(b-m)), derivative of val wrt b: 2n*p. Let's verify: F=2n/lam logsumexp(lam b) =2n[m + 1/lam logsumexp(lam(b-m))]. Derivative: 2n * lam/(lam) * p? derivative of logsumexp(lam(b-m)) = lam*sum p? Actually p normalized over scaled λ(b-m). d/d b_i of (1/λ)logsumexp(λ(b-m)) = p_i. Yes. Good.

`smooth_grad`: `corr = np.correlate(p, a, mode='valid')`, weighted derivative of C=∑ p_k b_k is 2 corr. F=2n*C, so gradA=4n corr. Good.

Now, if a sum=1 via softmax, gradient in z: `a*(gradA - dot(a, gradA))`. Correct because ∂a_i/∂z_j = a_i(δ_ij - a_j). So grad_z_j = ∑_i g_i a_j(δ_ij - a_j)=a_j(g_j - sum_i g_i a_i). Good.

Now, in `mirror_fb`, we use `g = gradA - dot(a,gradA)` and update `a *= exp(-eta*g)`. This approximates exp(-eta grad_z/a_i)? Actually update in z by -eta grad_z: z_i -= eta a_i(g_i - mean). Then a_i multiplies exp(eta a_i(...)) not exp(-eta(g_i-mean)). Our fallback uses directly `g`, not divided by a. That is different but okay heuristic. Could use `lf = -eta * g` as before. Good.

Now, `log_vector`: if many zeros, log eps and mean may be dominated; okay.

Now, potential issue: In `smooth_grad`, if `p` sum is 0 due to underflow, p onehot. If multiple max and lam infinite, scaled all -inf except max; if max set includes exp(0)=1 for each exact max? If b-m=0 for multiple, scaled 0, sum count. Good.

Let's write `deep_optimize` carefully:
```
def deep_optimize(a0, budget, holder, rng, label):
    a=normalize(a0)
    sc=safe_eval(a)
    update_holder(holder, a, sc, label)
    if budget <= 0: return a
    dl = time.monotonic() + budget
    n=len(a)
    if n <= 512 and time.monotonic() < dl:
        max_evals = 16 if n<=256 else 6
        a, sc = simple_moves(a, sc, holder, rng, max_evals=max_evals, label=label+" move")
    if HAVE_LINPROG and n <= 512 and time.monotonic() < dl - 2:
        lp_time = min(30.0, max(0.5, (dl - time.monotonic()) * 0.15))
        a = lp_improve(a, lp_time, holder, label+" lp")
    c_schedule = [0.1,0.3,1.0,3.0,10.0,30.0,100.0,300.0,1000.0,3000.0,10000.0]
    if time.monotonic() < dl - 2:
        maxit = 200 if n <= 128 else (120 if n <= 256 else (60 if n <= 512 else 25))
        a, sc = anneal_lbfgs(a, min( (dl-time.monotonic())*0.45, 600.0), holder, rng, label+" lbfgs", c_schedule, maxit)
    if n <= 512 and time.monotonic() < dl - 2:
        a, sc = simple_moves(a, sc, holder, rng, max_evals=12, label=label+" move2")
    if HAVE_LINPROG and n <= 512 and time.monotonic() < dl - 2:
        a = lp_improve(a, min(20.0, max(0.5, (dl-time.monotonic())*0.12)), holder, label+" lp2")
    if time.monotonic() < dl - 1:
        a = coordinate_descent(a, min(40.0, max(1.0, (dl-time.monotonic())*0.2)), holder, label+" coord")
    if time.monotonic() < dl - 5:
        a, sc = anneal_lbfgs(a, min((dl-time.monotonic())*0.3, 120.0), holder, rng, label+" high", [100.0,300.0,1000.0,3000.0,10000.0], 100 if n<=128 else 40)
    if n <= 256 and time.monotonic() < dl - 20:
        sigmas = [0.05,0.1,0.2,0.4]
        max_hops = 4
        for hp in range(max_hops):
            if time.monotonic() > dl - 20: break
            hops_left = max(1, max_hops - hp)
            hop_budget = min(60.0, max(5.0, (dl - time.monotonic() - 20.0) / hops_left))
            a_pert = add_noise(a, rng, sigmas[hp % len(sigmas)])
            a, sc = quick_optimize(a_pert, hop_budget, holder, rng, label+" hop")
    return a
```
But `quick_optimize` in basin hop may call simple_moves, LP, etc; could be heavy but okay. It may also call `anneal_lbfgs` with its own temps; maybe duplicate. Good.

`quick_optimize` should be less than deep. It uses simple moves max 6, LP, anneal. For hop, okay.

`quick_optimize`:
```
def quick_optimize(a0, budget, holder, rng, label):
    if budget <=0: return normalize(a0)
    a=normalize(a0); sc=safe_eval(a); update_holder(holder,a,sc,label)
    dl=time.monotonic()+budget
    n=len(a)
    if n <= 512 and time.monotonic() < dl:
        a, sc = simple_moves(a, sc, holder, rng, max_evals=6, label=label+" simple")
    if HAVE_LINPROG and n <= 256 and time.monotonic() < dl - 1:
        a = lp_improve(a, min(2.0, 0.2*budget, (dl-time.monotonic())), holder, label)
    if time.monotonic() < dl - 1:
        c_schedule=[0.3,3.0,30.0,300.0]
        maxit=60 if n<=128 else (30 if n<=256 else 15)
        a, sc = anneal_lbfgs(a, max(0.0, dl - time.monotonic()), holder, rng, label, c_schedule, maxit)
    if n <= 128 and time.monotonic() < dl - 1:
        a = coordinate_descent(a, min(2.0, (dl-time.monotonic())*0.2), holder, label+" coord")
    return a
```
Good.

def search_small(budget, rng):
    if budget <= 0: return None
    dl = time.monotonic()+budget
    holder=[float('inf'), None, ""]
    n=16
    cands=[]
    add_to_list(cands, np.ones(n))
    for p in [1.8,1.5,1.2,1.0,0.8,0.5,0.2]:
       add_to_list(cands, init_linear(n,p))
    for alpha in [-4,-2,2,4]: add_to_list(cands, init_exp(n,alpha))
    for gamma in [0.2,0.5,2,5]: add_to_list(cands, init_power(n,gamma,False)); add_to_list(cands, init_power(n,gamma,True))
    for a1,b1 in [(0.5,2),(2,0.5),(1,3),(3,1)]: add_to_list(cands, init_beta(n,a1,b1))
    for conc in [0.3,1.0,3.0]: add_to_list(cands, rng.dirichlet(np.ones(n)*conc))
    raws=[]
    for idx,a in enumerate(cands):
       if time.monotonic() > dl - 5: break
       sc=safe_eval(a)
       if np.isfinite(sc): raws.append((sc, idx, a))
    raws.sort()
    per = max(3.0, budget / max(1, min(8,len(raws))))
    for sc,idx,a in raws[:8]:
       if time.monotonic() > dl - 2: break
       t_alloc = min(per, dl - time.monotonic() - 1)
       if t_alloc < 1: break
       quick_optimize(a, t_alloc, holder, rng, "small")
    return holder[1]

```python
"""
Search for non-negative step-function heights minimizing

    2 * n * max_k (a * a)_k / (sum_j a_j)^2

where a*a is the full linear convolution.

The optimizer combines:
  - many diverse / interpolated initializers,
  - a small-n shape search,
  - linear-programming directions of the form
        maximize sum(g) subject to a*g <= max(a*a),
  - smoothed-logsumexp L-BFGS refinement with annealing,
  - simplex coordinate descent,
  - simple permutation / symmetry / blur moves,
  - basin hopping by multiplicative noise.

The function propose_candidate() runs for the available wall-clock budget
and returns the best sequence found.
"""

import time
import math

import numpy as np

try:
    from scipy.optimize import minimize
    HAVE_MINIMIZE = True
except Exception:
    minimize = None
    HAVE_MINIMIZE = False

try:
    from scipy.optimize import linprog
    HAVE_LINPROG = True
except Exception:
    linprog = None
    HAVE_LINPROG = False


def normalize(arr):
    """Clip to non-negative numbers and normalize to sum 1."""
    try:
        a = np.asarray(arr, dtype=float)
    except Exception:
        return np.ones(1)
    if a.ndim != 1:
        a = a.ravel()
    if a.size == 0:
        return np.ones(1)
    a = np.where(np.isfinite(a), a, 0.0)
    a = np.maximum(a, 0.0)
    s = float(a.sum())
    if not math.isfinite(s) or s <= 1e-15:
        return np.ones(a.size) / float(a.size)
    return a / s


def safe_eval(a):
    """Call the external evaluate_sequence on a normalized array."""
    try:
        arr = normalize(a)
        if arr.size == 0 or float(arr.sum()) < 0.01:
            return float("inf")
        val = evaluate_sequence([float(x) for x in arr])
        val = float(val)
        if math.isfinite(val):
            return val
    except Exception:
        pass
    return float("inf")


def update_holder(holder, cand, score, label):
    """Update holder = [best_score, best_array, best_label]."""
    try:
        score = float(score)
    except Exception:
        return
    if not math.isfinite(score):
        return
    if score < holder[0]:
        arr = np.asarray(cand, dtype=float)
        arr = np.where(np.isfinite(arr), arr, 0.0)
        arr = np.maximum(arr, 0.0)
        arr = np.minimum(arr, 1000.0)
        arr = normalize(arr)
        holder[0] = score
        holder[1] = arr.copy()
        holder[2] = label
        print("NEW BEST {:.8f}  len={}  {}".format(score, int(arr.size), label), flush=True)


def fft_conv(a):
    """Full linear auto-convolution by FFT, same order as np.convolve(a,a)."""
    a = np.asarray(a, dtype=float)
    n = int(a.size)
    if n == 0:
        return np.array([0.0], dtype=float)
    size = 2 * n - 1
    L = 1 << (size - 1).bit_length()
    A = np.fft.rfft(a, L)
    c = np.fft.irfft(A * A, L)[:size]
    c = np.where(np.isfinite(c), c, 0.0)
    c[c < 0.0] = 0.0
    return c


def fast_score(a):
    """Fast internal score for a normalized sequence."""
    arr = normalize(a)
    n = int(arr.size)
    if n == 0:
        return float("inf")
    b = fft_conv(arr)
    return 2.0 * n * float(b.max())


def fast_max_conv(a):
    """Fast internal max of convolution for a normalized sequence."""
    arr = normalize(a)
    if arr.size == 0:
        return 0.0
    return float(fft_conv(arr).max())


def softmax_z(z):
    """Stable softmax."""
    z = np.asarray(z, dtype=float)
    z = z - float(np.max(z))
    e = np.exp(z)
    s = float(e.sum())
    if not math.isfinite(s) or s <= 1e-300:
        return np.ones(z.size) / float(z.size)
    return e / s


def log_vector(a):
    """Log represented in the softmax coordinate system."""
    a = np.asarray(a, dtype=float)
    a = np.maximum(a, 1e-300)
    z = np.log(a)
    return z - float(np.mean(z))


def smooth_value(z, lam):
    """
    Smooth surrogate:
        2n * (1/lam) * log(sum exp(lam b_k))
    where b = convolution(a,a), a = softmax(z).
    """
    n = int(np.size(z))
    if lam is None or not math.isfinite(lam) or lam <= 0.0:
        a = softmax_z(z)
        return 2.0 * n * float(fft_conv(a).max())
    a = softmax_z(z)
    b = fft_conv(a)
    m = float(b.max())
    scaled = lam * (b - m)
    exp_scaled = np.exp(scaled)
    s = float(exp_scaled.sum())
    if s <= 0.0 or not math.isfinite(s):
        lse = 0.0
    else:
        lse = math.log(s)
    return 2.0 * n * (m + lse / lam)


def smooth_grad(z, lam):
    """Gradient of smooth_value with respect to the softmax logits z."""
    n = int(np.size(z))
    if lam is None or not math.isfinite(lam) or lam <= 0.0:
        return np.zeros_like(z)
    a = softmax_z(z)
    b = fft_conv(a)
    m = float(b.max())
    scaled = lam * (b - m)
    exp_scaled = np.exp(scaled)
    s = float(exp_scaled.sum())
    if s > 0.0 and math.isfinite(s):
        p = exp_scaled / s
    else:
        p = np.zeros_like(b)
        p[int(np.argmax(b))] = 1.0

    # For b_k = sum_i a_i a_{k-i}, d(sum p b)/da_j = 2 * sum_l p_{j+l} a_l.
    # The objective has an additional factor 2n.
    grad_a = 4.0 * n * np.correlate(p, a, mode="valid")
    grad_a = np.asarray(grad_a, dtype=float)
    if grad_a.size != n:
        grad_a = grad_a[:n]

    mean_g = float(np.dot(a, grad_a))
    grad_z = a * (grad_a - mean_g)
    return np.where(np.isfinite(grad_z), grad_z, 0.0)


def mirror_fb(a, lam, steps=20):
    """Fallback multiplicative natural-gradient steps if scipy is unavailable."""
    a = normalize(a)
    n = int(a.size)
    for _ in range(int(steps)):
        b = fft_conv(a)
        m = float(b.max())
        if m <= 1e-300:
            break
        scaled = lam * (b - m)
        exp_scaled = np.exp(scaled)
        s = float(exp_scaled.sum())
        if s > 0.0 and math.isfinite(s):
            p = exp_scaled / s
        else:
            p = np.zeros_like(b)
            p[int(np.argmax(b))] = 1.0
        corr = np.correlate(p, a, mode="valid")
        corr = corr[:n]
        g = 4.0 * n * corr
        g = g - float(np.dot(a, g))
        lf = -0.08 * g
        lf = lf - float(np.max(lf))
        a = a * np.exp(lf)
        a = normalize(a)
    return a


def lbfgs_step(a, lam, maxiter=100):
    """One L-BFGS (or fallback) optimization in softmax coordinates."""
    a = normalize(a)
    if maxiter <= 0:
        return a
    if HAVE_MINIMIZE:
        z = log_vector(a)
        try:
            opts = {
                "maxiter": int(maxiter),
                "ftol": 1e-12,
                "gtol": 1e-8,
                "maxcor": 20,
                "maxls": 50,
            }
            res = minimize(
                smooth_value,
                z,
                method="L-BFGS-B",
                jac=smooth_grad,
                args=(lam,),
                options=opts,
            )
            if res is not None and res.x is not None:
                a2 = softmax_z(res.x)
                if a2.size == a.size:
                    return a2
        except Exception:
            pass
    return mirror_fb(a, lam, steps=25)


def get_parent():
    """Access the previously supplied construction, if available."""
    try:
        p = np.asarray(height_sequence_1, dtype=float)
        p = np.where(np.isfinite(p), p, 0.0)
        p = np.maximum(p, 0.0)
        if p.ndim == 1 and p.size > 0 and float(p.sum()) > 1e-15:
            return p
    except Exception:
        pass
    return None


def init_linear(n, p):
    """Linear skew with left endpoint p and right endpoint 2-p."""
    if n <= 1:
        return np.ones(n)
    q = 2.0 - p
    a = np.linspace(p, q, n)
    a = np.clip(a, 0.0, None)
    if float(a.sum()) <= 1e-15:
        a = np.linspace(1.0, float(n), n)
    return normalize(a)


def init_exp(n, alpha):
    if n <= 1:
        return np.ones(n)
    x = np.linspace(0.0, 1.0, n)
    a = np.exp(alpha * (x - 0.5))
    return normalize(a)


def init_power(n, gamma, rev=False):
    if n <= 1:
        return np.ones(n)
    x = np.linspace(1e-3, 1.0, n)
    a = np.power(x, gamma)
    if rev:
        a = a[::-1]
    return normalize(a)


def init_beta(n, a1, b1):
    if n <= 2:
        return np.ones(n)
    x = np.linspace(1e-4, 1.0 - 1e-4, n)
    a = (x ** (a1 - 1.0)) * ((1.0 - x) ** (b1 - 1.0))
    a = np.where(np.isfinite(a), a, 0.0)
    a = np.maximum(a, 0.0)
    if float(a.sum()) <= 1e-15:
        a = np.ones(n)
    return normalize(a)


def parent_to_length(parent, n):
    """Interpolate or truncate a parent shape to length n."""
    p = normalize(parent)
    if p.size == n:
        return p
    if p.size == 1:
        return np.full(n, float(p[0]))
    xold = np.linspace(0.0, 1.0, p.size)
    xnew = np.linspace(0.0, 1.0, n)
    q = np.interp(xnew, xold, p)
    return normalize(q)


def add_to_list(lst, arr):
    if arr is None:
        return
    a = normalize(arr)
    if a.size > 0 and bool(np.all(np.isfinite(a))):
        lst.append(a)


def generate_initial_candidates(parent, rng):
    """Produce a broad set of initial sequences at several lengths."""
    cands = []

    if parent is not None:
        add_to_list(cands, parent)
        for n in [64, 128, 256, 512, 1024, 2048]:
            add_to_list(cands, parent_to_length(parent, n))

    for n in [64, 128, 256, 512]:
        add_to_list(cands, np.ones(n))

        for p in [1.8, 1.6, 1.4, 1.2, 1.0, 0.8, 0.6, 0.4, 0.2]:
            add_to_list(cands, init_linear(n, p))

        for alpha in [-4.0, -2.0, 2.0, 4.0]:
            add_to_list(cands, init_exp(n, alpha))

        for gamma in [0.2, 0.5, 2.0, 5.0]:
            add_to_list(cands, init_power(n, gamma, False))
            add_to_list(cands, init_power(n, gamma, True))

        for a1, b1 in [(0.5, 2.0), (2.0, 0.5), (0.8, 1.5), (1.5, 0.8), (1.0, 3.0), (3.0, 1.0)]:
            add_to_list(cands, init_beta(n, a1, b1))

        for conc in [0.25, 1.0, 5.0]:
            add_to_list(cands, rng.dirichlet(np.ones(n) * conc))

    for n in [1024, 2048]:
        add_to_list(cands, np.ones(n))
        for p in [1.6, 1.3, 0.7, 0.4]:
            add_to_list(cands, init_linear(n, p))
        for alpha in [-3.0, 3.0]:
            add_to_list(cands, init_exp(n, alpha))
        add_to_list(cands, rng.dirichlet(np.ones(n)))
        add_to_list(cands, rng.dirichlet(np.ones(n) * 0.2))

    return cands


def blur1d(a):
    if len(a) < 3:
        return normalize(a)
    k = np.array([1.0, 2.0, 1.0], dtype=float)
    b = np.convolve(a, k, mode="same")
    return normalize(b)


def zigzag(a):
    """A simple alternating arrangement of sorted values."""
    a = normalize(a)
    n = len(a)
    d = np.sort(a)[::-1]
    out = np.zeros(n, dtype=float)
    ev = np.arange(0, n, 2)
    od = np.arange(1, n, 2)
    ke = min(len(ev), len(d))
    ko = min(len(od), max(0, len(d) - ke))
    out[ev[:ke]] = d[:ke]
    out[od[:ko]] = d[ke:ke + ko]
    return normalize(out)


def simple_moves(a, score, holder, rng, max_evals=10, label="move"):
    """Try deterministic and random reorder / smoothing moves."""
    a = normalize(a)
    best = a.copy()
    best_sc = float(score)
    update_holder(holder, best, best_sc, label)
    evals = 0
    n = len(a)

    moves = []
    if n > 1:
        moves.append(a[::-1])
        moves.append(0.5 * (a + a[::-1]))
    if n > 2:
        moves.append(blur1d(a))
    s = np.sort(a)
    moves.extend([s, s[::-1], zigzag(a)])

    for raw in moves:
        if evals >= max_evals:
            break
        cand = normalize(raw)
        evals += 1
        sc = safe_eval(cand)
        if math.isfinite(sc) and sc < best_sc:
            best = cand
            best_sc = sc
            update_holder(holder, best, best_sc, label)

    if rng is not None:
        while evals < max_evals:
            cand = a.copy()
            rng.shuffle(cand)
            cand = normalize(cand)
            evals += 1
            sc = safe_eval(cand)
            if math.isfinite(sc) and sc < best_sc:
                best = cand
                best_sc = sc
                update_holder(holder, best, best_sc, label + " perm")
            if n > 1024 and evals >= 4:
                break

    return best, best_sc


def add_noise(a, rng, sigma):
    """Multiplicative noise in log-space, preserving sum 1."""
    a = normalize(a)
    z = np.log(np.maximum(a, 1e-300))
    z += rng.normal(0.0, float(sigma), size=z.size)
    return softmax_z(z)


def anneal_lbfgs(a, budget, holder, rng, label, c_schedule, maxiter_base):
    """Anneal smooth surrogates from mild focusing to very sharp focusing."""
    a = normalize(a)
    if budget <= 0.0:
        return a, safe_eval(a)
    deadline = time.monotonic() + float(budget)

    best = a.copy()
    best_sc = safe_eval(best)
    update_holder(holder, best, best_sc, label + " init")

    cur = a.copy()
    fast_cur = fast_score(cur)
    fast_best = fast_score(best)

    for c in c_schedule:
        if time.monotonic() > deadline:
            break
        if c <= 0.0:
            continue
        bmax = fast_max_conv(cur)
        if bmax <= 1e-18:
            break
        lam = c / max(bmax, 1e-18)
        lam = min(float(lam), 1e8)

        start = cur
        if fast_best < fast_cur:
            start = best

        mean_start = float(np.mean(start))
        if mean_start > 1e-15 and float(np.max(start)) / mean_start < 1.02:
            start = add_noise(start, rng, 0.12)

        maxit = int(maxiter_base)
        a2 = lbfgs_step(start, lam, maxit)
        a2 = normalize(a2)
        sc2 = safe_eval(a2)

        if math.isfinite(sc2) and sc2 < best_sc:
            best = a2
            best_sc = sc2
            cur = a2
            fast_cur = fast_score(cur)
            fast_best = fast_score(best)
            update_holder(holder, best, best_sc, label + " c={:g}".format(c))
        else:
            f2 = fast_score(a2)
            if f2 < fast_cur:
                cur = a2
                fast_cur = f2

    return best, best_sc


def lp_direction(a):
    """
    Solve LP:
        maximize sum(h)
        subject to conv(a,h) <= max(conv(a,a)), h >= 0.
    Return normalized h, or None on failure.
    """
    if not HAVE_LINPROG:
        return None

    a = normalize(a)
    n = int(a.size)
    if n <= 1:
        return None
    M = fast_max_conv(a)
    if not math.isfinite(M) or M <= 1e-300:
        return None

    m = 2 * n - 1
    C = np.zeros((m, n), dtype=float)
    for j in range(n):
        C[j:j + n, j] = a

    A = C / M
    rowsum = np.sum(A, axis=1)
    keep = rowsum > 1e-14
    if not np.all(keep):
        A = A[keep]
    if A.size == 0:
        return None

    try:
        res = linprog(
            c=-np.ones(n),
            A_ub=A,
            b_ub=np.ones(A.shape[0]),
            bounds=[(0.0, None)] * n,
            method="highs",
            options={"disp": False},
        )
        if res is None or not getattr(res, "success", False):
            return None
        h = np.asarray(res.x, dtype=float)
        h = np.where(np.isfinite(h), h, 0.0)
        h = np.maximum(h, 0.0)
        s = float(h.sum())
        if s <= 1e-15:
            return None
        return h / s
    except Exception:
        return None


def lp_improve(a, budget, holder, label="lp"):
    """Repeat LP direction + line search while it improves."""
    a = normalize(a)
    sc = safe_eval(a)
    update_holder(holder, a, sc, label)
    if budget <= 0.0 or not HAVE_LINPROG:
        return a
    deadline = time.monotonic() + float(budget)

    max_iter = 50
    for _ in range(int(max_iter)):
        if time.monotonic() > deadline:
            break
        h = lp_direction(a)
        if h is None:
            break

        best_a = a.copy()
        best_sc = sc
        cur = time.monotonic()
        for t in [0.05, 0.1, 0.2, 0.35, 0.5, 0.7, 0.9, 1.0]:
            if time.monotonic() > deadline:
                break
            cand = (1.0 - t) * a + t * h
            cand = normalize(cand)
            scand = safe_eval(cand)
            if math.isfinite(scand) and scand < best_sc:
                best_a = cand
                best_sc = scand

        if best_sc < sc - 1e-14:
            a = best_a
            sc = best_sc
            update_holder(holder, a, sc, label)
        else:
            # If no improvement, try one very small step before stopping.
            cand = 0.98 * a + 0.02 * h
            cand = normalize(cand)
            scand = safe_eval(cand)
            if math.isfinite(scand) and scand < sc - 1e-14:
                a = cand
                sc = scand
                update_holder(holder, a, sc, label + " tiny")
            else:
                break
    return a


def coordinate_descent(a, budget, holder, label="coord"):
    """One-coordinate simplex line search using the true evaluator."""
    a = normalize(a)
    if budget <= 0.0:
        return a
    n = len(a)
    if n == 0:
        return np.ones(1)
    sc = safe_eval(a)
    best = a.copy()
    best_sc = sc
    update_holder(holder, best, best_sc, label)

    deadline = time.monotonic() + float(budget)

    if n <= 128:
        ratios = [0.0, 0.35, 0.65, 1.35, 1.8, 2.5]
        max_sweeps = 60
    elif n <= 256:
        ratios = [0.0, 0.5, 1.5]
        max_sweeps = 18
    elif n <= 512:
        ratios = [0.0, 0.5]
        max_sweeps = 8
    else:
        return best

    sweeps = 0
    while sweeps < max_sweeps and time.monotonic() < deadline:
        improved = False
        for i in range(n):
            if time.monotonic() > deadline:
                break
            old = float(best[i])
            other = 1.0 - old
            candidates = [0.0] + [old * r for r in ratios] + [1.0]
            for x in candidates:
                if time.monotonic() > deadline:
                    break
                x = float(x)
                if not math.isfinite(x):
                    continue
                if x < 0.0:
                    x = 0.0
                if x > 1.0:
                    x = 1.0
                if abs(x - old) <= 1e-13:
                    continue
                if other <= 1e-12:
                    if x < 1.0 - 1e-12:
                        continue
                    temp = np.zeros(n, dtype=float)
                    temp[i] = 1.0
                else:
                    temp = best.copy()
                    temp *= (1.0 - x) / other
                    temp[i] = x
                scand = safe_eval(temp)
                if math.isfinite(scand) and scand < best_sc - 1e-14:
                    best = temp
                    best_sc = scand
                    improved = True
                    update_holder(holder, best, best_sc, label)
        sweeps += 1
        if not improved:
            break
    return best


def quick_optimize(a0, budget, holder, rng, label="quick"):
    """Moderate local optimizer used for scanning and hops."""
    if budget <= 0.0:
        return normalize(a0)
    a = normalize(a0)
    sc = safe_eval(a)
    update_holder(holder, a, sc, label)
    deadline = time.monotonic() + float(budget)
    n = len(a)

    if n <= 512 and time.monotonic() < deadline:
        a, sc = simple_moves(a, sc, holder, rng, max_evals=6, label=label + " simple")

    if HAVE_LINPROG and n <= 256 and time.monotonic() < deadline - 1.0:
        lp_time = min(2.5, 0.25 * budget, max(0.0, deadline - time.monotonic()))
        a = lp_improve(a, lp_time, holder, label + " lp")

    if time.monotonic() < deadline - 1.0:
        c_schedule = [0.3, 3.0, 30.0, 300.0]
        maxit = 60 if n <= 128 else (35 if n <= 256 else 18)
        a, sc = anneal_lbfgs(
            a,
            max(0.0, deadline - time.monotonic()),
            holder,
            rng,
            label + " lbfgs",
            c_schedule,
            maxit,
        )

    if n <= 128 and time.monotonic() < deadline - 0.5:
        a = coordinate_descent(
            a,
            min(2.0, 0.15 * budget, max(0.0, deadline - time.monotonic())),
            holder,
            label + " coord",
        )

    return a


def deep_optimize(a0, budget, holder, rng, label="deep"):
    """Longer local/global optimizer for the best candidates."""
    if budget <= 0.0:
        return normalize(a0)
    a = normalize(a0)
    sc = safe_eval(a)
    update_holder(holder, a, sc, label)
    deadline = time.monotonic() + float(budget)
    n = len(a)

    if n <= 512 and time.monotonic() < deadline:
        max_evals = 16 if n <= 256 else 6
        a, sc = simple_moves(a, sc, holder, rng, max_evals=max_evals, label=label + " move1")

    if HAVE_LINPROG and n <= 512 and time.monotonic() < deadline - 2.0:
        lp_time = min(35.0, max(0.5, 0.14 * (deadline - time.monotonic())))
        a = lp_improve(a, lp_time, holder, label + " lp1")

    c_schedule = [0.1, 0.3, 1.0, 3.0, 10.0, 30.0, 100.0, 300.0, 1000.0, 3000.0, 10000.0]
    if time.monotonic() < deadline - 2.0:
        maxit = 220 if n <= 128 else (120 if n <= 256 else (60 if n <= 512 else 25))
        budget1 = min(600.0, 0.42 * (deadline - time.monotonic()))
        a, sc = anneal_lbfgs(a, budget1, holder, rng, label + " lbfgs1", c_schedule, maxit)

    if n <= 512 and time.monotonic() < deadline - 2.0:
        a, sc = simple_moves(a, sc, holder, rng, max_evals=12, label=label + " move2")

    if HAVE_LINPROG and n <= 512 and time.monotonic() < deadline - 2.0:
        lp_time = min(25.0, max(0.5, 0.10 * (deadline - time.monotonic())))
        a = lp_improve(a, lp_time, holder, label + " lp2")

    if time.monotonic() < deadline - 1.0:
        a = coordinate_descent(
            a,
            min(45.0, 0.18 * (deadline - time.monotonic()), budget),
            holder,
            label + " coord",
        )

    if time.monotonic() < deadline - 5.0:
        high_schedule = [100.0, 300.0, 1000.0, 3000.0, 10000.0]
        maxit = 120 if n <= 128 else (60 if n <= 256 else 25)
        budget2 = min(160.0, 0.30 * (deadline - time.monotonic()))
        a, sc = anneal_lbfgs(a, budget2, holder, rng, label + " high", high_schedule, maxit)

    if n <= 256 and time.monotonic() < deadline - 25.0:
        sigmas = [0.05, 0.10, 0.20, 0.40]
        max_hops = 4
        for hp in range(int(max_hops)):
            if time.monotonic() > deadline - 25.0:
                break
            hops_left = max(1, int(max_hops) - hp)
            hop_budget = min(70.0, max(5.0, (deadline - time.monotonic() - 25.0) / float(hops_left)))
            a_pert = add_noise(a, rng, sigmas[hp % len(sigmas)])
            a, sc = quick_optimize(a_pert, hop_budget, holder, rng, label + " hop")

    return a


def search_small(budget, rng):
    """Quickly find a good short sequence, then interpolate it later."""
    if budget <= 2.0:
        return None
    deadline = time.monotonic() + float(budget)
    holder = [float("inf"), None, ""]
    n = 16
    cands = []

    add_to_list(cands, np.ones(n))
    for p in [1.8, 1.5, 1.2, 1.0, 0.8, 0.5, 0.2]:
        add_to_list(cands, init_linear(n, p))
    for alpha in [-4.0, -2.0, 2.0, 4.0]:
        add_to_list(cands, init_exp(n, alpha))
    for gamma in [0.2, 0.5, 2.0, 5.0]:
        add_to_list(cands, init_power(n, gamma, False))
        add_to_list(cands, init_power(n, gamma, True))
    for a1, b1 in [(0.5, 2.0), (2.0, 0.5), (0.8, 1.5), (1.5, 0.8), (1.0, 3.0), (3.0, 1.0)]:
        add_to_list(cands, init_beta(n, a1, b1))
    for conc in [0.25, 1.0, 3.0]:
        add_to_list(cands, rng.dirichlet(np.ones(n) * conc))

    raws = []
    for idx, a in enumerate(cands):
        if time.monotonic() > deadline - 3.0:
            break
        sc = safe_eval(a)
        if math.isfinite(sc):
            raws.append((sc, idx, a))
    raws.sort()

    k = max(1, min(8, len(raws)))
    per = max(2.0, budget / float(k))
    for sc, idx, a in raws[:k]:
        if time.monotonic() > deadline - 1.0:
            break
        alloc = min(per, max(0.0, deadline - time.monotonic() - 0.5))
        if alloc < 1.0:
            break
        quick_optimize(a, alloc, holder, rng, "small")

    if holder[0] < float("inf"):
        return holder[1]
    return None


def propose_candidate(seed=42, budget_s=2400, **kwargs):
    """
    Main entry point. Runs the combined search and returns the best sequence.
    """
    start = time.monotonic()
    try:
        wall = min(float(budget_s), 2400.0)
    except Exception:
        wall = 2400.0
    # Leave a safety margin to ensure we return before external termination.
    deadline = start + max(120.0, wall - 120.0)

    rng = np.random.default_rng(seed)
    holder = [float("inf"), None, ""]

    parent = get_parent()
    if parent is not None:
        sc_parent = safe_eval(parent)
        update_holder(holder, parent, sc_parent, "parent")

    # Very short preliminary shape search.
    if time.monotonic() < deadline - 360.0:
        small_budget = min(60.0, max(5.0, deadline - 360.0 - time.monotonic()))
        small_best = search_small(small_budget, rng)
        if small_best is not None:
            sc_small = safe_eval(small_best)
            update_holder(holder, small_best, sc_small, "small best")
            small_holder_for_candidates = [float("inf"), None, ""]
            # Do not optimize small_best again here; interpolated versions are used below.
            small_holder_for_candidates[1] = small_best.copy()

        # Candidate pool.
        cands = generate_initial_candidates(parent, rng)
        if small_best is not None:
            for n in [64, 128, 256, 512, 1024, 2048]:
                add_to_list(cands, parent_to_length(small_best, n))
    else:
        cands = generate_initial_candidates(parent, rng)

    # Raw evaluation of candidate pool.
    raw_infos = []
    for idx, a in enumerate(cands):
        if len(raw_infos) >= 220:
            break
        if time.monotonic() > deadline - 420.0:
            break
        sc = safe_eval(a)
        if math.isfinite(sc):
            raw_infos.append((sc, idx, a))
    raw_infos.sort()

    selected = []
    seen = set()
    for sc, idx, a in raw_infos:
        if len(selected) >= 16:
            break
        if idx in seen:
            continue
        selected.append((sc, a))
        seen.add(idx)

    # Ensure the parent is represented quickly.
    if parent is not None and len(selected) < 20:
        p = normalize(parent)
        dup = False
        for _sc, aa in selected:
            if aa.size == p.size and np.allclose(aa, p, rtol=1e-3, atol=1e-6):
                dup = True
                break
        if not dup:
            selected.append((safe_eval(p), p))

    # Ensure the best small-ish shape is represented.
    if holder[1] is not None and len(selected) < 20:
        best_len = len(holder[1])
        has_best_len = False
        for _sc, aa in selected:
            if aa.size == best_len:
                has_best_len = True
                break
        if not has_best_len:
            selected.append((holder[0], holder[1].copy()))

    if not selected:
        fallback = np.ones(128)
        selected.append((safe_eval(fallback), fallback))

    # Quick optimization of the best raw candidates.
    deep_results = []
    quick_deadline = deadline - 320.0
    quick_total = max(0.0, quick_deadline - time.monotonic())
    if selected and quick_total > 5.0:
        t_per = max(3.0, min(25.0, quick_total / float(len(selected))))
        for i, (sc, a) in enumerate(selected):
            if time.monotonic() > quick_deadline - 2.0:
                break
            alloc = min(t_per, max(0.0, quick_deadline - time.monotonic() - 2.0))
            if alloc < 1.5:
                break
            arr_ret = quick_optimize(a, alloc, holder, rng, "quick")
            if arr_ret is not None:
                sc_ret = safe_eval(arr_ret)
                if math.isfinite(sc_ret):
                    deep_results.append((sc_ret, i, arr_ret.copy()))

    if holder[1] is not None:
        deep_results.append((holder[0], -1, holder[1].copy()))

    deep_results.sort()

    deep_candidates = []
    for sc, idx, a in deep_results[:6]:
        deep_candidates.append(a.copy())

    has_128 = False
    for a in deep_candidates:
        if len(a) == 128:
            has_128 = True
            break
    if not has_128:
        for sc, idx, a in deep_results:
            if len(a) == 128:
                deep_candidates.append(a.copy())
                break

    if holder[1] is not None:
        deep_candidates.append(holder[1].copy())

    # Deep optimization of the most promising candidates.
    final_reserve = 230.0
    if deep_candidates:
        rem = max(0.0, deadline - final_reserve - 20.0 - time.monotonic())
        if rem > 30.0:
            count = max(1, int(rem / 280.0))
            count = min(count, len(deep_candidates), 6)
            t_per = max(25.0, rem / float(count))
            for a in deep_candidates[:count]:
                if time.monotonic() > deadline - final_reserve - 20.0:
                    break
                alloc = min(t_per, max(0.0, deadline - final_reserve - 20.0 - time.monotonic()))
                if alloc < 10.0:
                    break
                deep_optimize(a, alloc, holder, rng, "deep")

    # Final polish.
    if holder[1] is not None:
        final_budget = max(0.0, deadline - 8.0 - time.monotonic())
        final_budget = min(215.0, final_budget)
        if final_budget > 8.0:
            deep_optimize(holder[1].copy(), final_budget, holder, rng, "final polish")

    # Ensure a valid return.
    if holder[1] is None:
        return [float(1.0 / 128.0) for _ in range(128)]

    arr = np.asarray(holder[1], dtype=float)
    arr = np.where(np.isfinite(arr), arr, 0.0)
    arr = np.maximum(arr, 0.0)
    arr = np.minimum(arr, 1000.0)
    arr = normalize(arr)

    if arr.size == 0:
        return [float(1.0 / 128.0) for _ in range(128)]

    final_score = holder[0]
    print("FINAL SCORE {:.8f}".format(final_score), flush=True)
    return [float(x) for x in arr]
```
